- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`tests/designer-eval/src/score.ts`:

```ts
export type Side = "left" | "right";
export type Choice = Side | "none";

/** One-sided exact binomial tail P(X ≥ k) for n fair coin flips. */
function binomialTail(k: number, n: number): number {
  let p = 0;
  let c = 1;
  for (let i = 0; i <= n; i += 1) {
    if (i >= k) p += c;
    c = (c * (n - i)) / (i + 1);
  }
  return p / 2 ** n;
}

export const BAR = { winRate: 0.6, minDecided: 20 } as const;

export function score(results: Record<string, Choice>, key: Record<string, Side>) {
  let decided = 0;
  let modelWins = 0;
  let ties = 0;
  for (const [pair, choice] of Object.entries(results)) {
    if (!(pair in key)) continue;
    if (choice === "none") ties += 1;
    else {
      decided += 1;
      if (choice === key[pair]) modelWins += 1;
    }
  }
  const winRate = decided === 0 ? 0 : modelWins / decided;
  return {
    decided,
    modelWins,
    ties,
    winRate,
    pValue: binomialTail(modelWins, decided),
    passes: decided >= BAR.minDecided && winRate >= BAR.winRate,
  };
}
```

`pick.ts`, `render.ts` and `compare-page.ts` follow the protocol above:
- `pick.ts`: `createDb(process.env.DATABASE_URL)`, `loadStructureBlocks`, `summarize`, `planDesign`, `refineDesign`, and `createOpenAI({ apiKey: process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY })` through `createDesignerModel`. The key is read from the environment only, and never printed.
- `render.ts`: `@playwright/test`'s `chromium` and the bench `app-client` sign-in.
- `compare-page.ts`: writes one static HTML file, with inline CSS, about 30 lines of inline JS for the keys and the Blob download, and only relative `img` sources.

Document the run in `tests/designer-eval/README.md`. The commands are run by the orchestrator, never by CI:

```
DATABASE_URL=… OPENAI_API_KEY=… pnpm --filter @mastertutor/designer-eval pick
DATABASE_URL=… APP_URL=… OWNER_EMAIL=… OWNER_PASSWORD=… pnpm --filter @mastertutor/designer-eval render
open tests/designer-eval/out/<seed>/compare.html
pnpm --filter @mastertutor/designer-eval score out/<seed>/results.json out/<seed>/key.json
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run the test, then the evaluation**

Run: `scripts/remote-test.sh unit tests/designer-eval`. Expected: PASS.

Then the orchestrator runs the protocol once on the D47 stack. It spends at most $1 of real API. It writes `orchestration/runs/<date>-designer-eval/report.md` with the corpus by source kind, decided and tied pairs, the win rate, the p-value and the spend. It reports these to the user **before** any default change.

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

```bash
git commit -m "test(observer): blind side-by-side designer evaluation harness with a 60% bar (D53b)

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01VBPg8LB4dBTSYr8jHzmPLg" -- tests/designer-eval scripts/README.md .gitignore pnpm-workspace.yaml
```

Only after the user approves a passing result:

```bash
git commit -m "feat(observer): document designer model path on by default after the blind evaluation (D53b)

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01VBPg8LB4dBTSYr8jHzmPLg" -- packages/contracts/src/env.ts packages/contracts/src/env.test.ts
```

## Execution handoff

Execution is subagent-driven on Opus 5.5 (D30, D35, D40), with a fresh reviewer after each task and a whole-branch review at the end:

1. **Foundation:** one implementer runs Task 0, and a second runs Task 1 (the spike) in parallel. Then Tasks 2–5 run in order. The Foundation track merges as one unit after Task 5 and a green `scripts/remote-test.sh all`.
2. **Tracks G, C and U** start together, one implementer each, in their own worktrees.
3. **Track E** starts when G5 has merged. E3 waits for C6, and E4 for G8, C7 and U4.
4. **Track D** starts when F and notes Tasks 0.1–0.2 have merged. D8 waits for the notes reader. It has one implementer.
5. **First zyBooks run with the Guard:** it runs in shadow (G8's bench default). E2 calibrates on its corpus before any later run enforces.

The real `observer-eval` run (E4), the designer evaluation (D8) and the first shadow zyBooks run each spend real money: up to $2, up to $1, and the D46 run budget. The designer's model path stays off until the user has seen D8's result. The orchestrator reports both results to the user before the Guard is switched to enforce for benchmark runs.
